## Campaign issues

### Campaign themes



Presidential candidates [Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign) and [Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign) campaigned in 17 states, excluding their home states, from August to November in the 2024 United States presidential election.

[^cite_note-216]

See also: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Platform), [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Platform), and [2024 United States elections § Issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_elections#Issues)

#### Harris campaign

Harris framed her campaign as "a choice between freedom and chaos" and based it around the ideals of "freedom" and "the future".[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218] The Harris campaign sought to highlight her experience as an attorney general and a prosecutor to "prosecute the case" against Trump by pointing out his 34 felony convictions and the impacts of the overturning of *[Roe v. Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade)*.[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220] Harris had taken liberal positions on a number of issues in her bid for the 2020 Democratic nomination; in 2024, she shifted several of those positions toward the political center and embraced many of Biden's domestic policy stances.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-7-221] Harris focused her economic proposals on the cost of [groceries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grocery_prices_\(greedflation\)), [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_crisis_in_the_United_States) and [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_prices_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-222]

#### Trump campaign

See also: [Trumpism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trumpism) and [Rhetoric of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhetoric_of_Donald_Trump)

A central campaign theme for Trump's second presidential bid was "retribution".[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224] Trump framed the 2024 election as "the final battle", and openly promised to leverage the power of the presidency for [political reprisals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Investigation_and_arrest_of_political_rivals).[^cite_note-225] Trump heavily ran on immigration as a central campaign focus. Trump's campaign focused on dark and apocalyptic rhetoric about the state of the country and predicting doom if he did not win.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227][^cite_note-Parker_03172024-228] The [Associated Press](18935732.md) stated that "Trump's rallies take on the symbols, rhetoric and agenda of [Christian nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_nationalism_in_the_United_States)."[^cite_note-229] During his 2024 presidential campaign, Trump made numerous [false and misleading statements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_or_misleading_statements_by_Donald_Trump#Campaign_for_2024_presidency).[^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Times-2024-230][^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2024-2-231][^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2024-232] Trump has been described as using the "[big lie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_lie)"[^cite_note-233] and [firehose of falsehood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firehose_of_falsehood)[^cite_note-234] propaganda techniques.

### Abortion

Main article: [Abortion in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_in_the_United_States)



[Abortion-rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_abortion-rights_movement) protesters in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) in May 2022, as part of the Bans Off Our Bodies protest following the [leaked draft opinion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaked_draft_opinion_in_Dobbs_v._Jackson_Women's_Health_Organization) overturning *[Roe v. Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade)*



Trump speaking at the 2020 [March for Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_for_Life_\(Washington,_D.C.\)) in Washington, D.C.

[Abortion](765.md) access was a key topic during the campaign;[^cite_note-heres-why-abortion-235][^cite_note-NBC-2024-236] it was on the ballot in up to 10 states in 2024, including the [swing states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state) of [Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Arizona_Proposition_139) and Nevada.[^cite_note-237] Abortion was a key issue for many voters in the [2022 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_United_States_elections).[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] The issue continued to motivate voters in 2024 along with "the future of democracy in this country" and "high prices for gas, groceries and other goods." According to AP VoteCast, 25% of voters ranked abortion policy as the single most important factor in their vote, similar to the share who in 2022 said that the Supreme Court overturning Roe v. Wade was important to their vote. Of the states where abortion was on the ballot, measures seeking to expand or protect abortion access failed in Florida, South Dakota and Nebraska.[^cite_note-240]

Some pundits argued abortion rights referendums could help Harris in November.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243] Democrats predominantly advocate for [abortion access as a right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_abortion-rights_movement),[^cite_note-244] while Republicans generally favor significantly [restricting the legality of abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_anti-abortion_movement).[^cite_note-245] Since becoming the presumptive nominee, Harris indicated her support for passing legislation which would restore the federal abortion right protections previously guaranteed by *Roe*.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] She argued Trump would let his anti-abortion allies implement [Project 2025 proposals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_2025_and_women's_reproductive_health) to restrict abortion and contraception throughout the United States.[^cite_note-248]

Trump claimed credit for overturning *Roe* but criticized Republicans pushing for total abortion bans.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] Trump said he would leave the issue of abortion for the states to decide but would allow red states to monitor women's pregnancies and prosecute them if they have an abortion.[^cite_note-Time-2024-251] In his home state of Florida, Trump announced he would vote "No" on [Amendment 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Florida_Amendment_4), an abortion rights referendum, preserving the six-week ban.[^cite_note-252] The announcement came one day after he initially criticized the six-week ban for being "too short" and said he would vote to lengthen it.[^cite_note-253] Trump repeated a false claim that Democrats support abortions after birth and "executing" babies.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255]

### Border security and immigration

Main articles: [Mexico–United States border crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border_crisis) and [Border security in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Border_security_in_the_United_States)

Further information: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Immigration), and [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Immigration)



Harris as [Attorney General of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_California) at the border in 2011 to discuss strategies to combat transnational gang crime

Border security and immigration were among the top issues concerning potential voters in the election.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-democracy-2024-election-257] Polling showed that most Americans want to reduce immigration,[^cite_note-258] and that a substantial minority of white Republicans were concerned about [white demographic decline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_demographic_decline).[^cite_note-259] In 2023 and early 2024, a surge of migrants entering through the border with Mexico occurred.[^cite_note-260] By June 2024, illegal crossings reached a three-year low following four consecutive monthly drops, which senior officials attributed to increased enforcement between the United States and Mexico, the weather, and Biden's executive order ([A Proclamation on Securing the Border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Proclamation_on_Securing_the_Border)) increasing asylum restrictions.[^cite_note-261]

Harris promised to fight for "strong border security" coupled with an earned pathway to citizenship. Harris highlighted her work in combating transnational gangs, drug cartels, and [human traffickers](19333662.md) while attorney general.[^cite_note-Politico-2024-262] As vice president, Harris announced in 2023 that she had garnered pledges of US\$950 million from private companies to aid Central American communities to address the causes of mass migration, such as poverty.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] Harris stated she believes the immigration system is "broken" and needs to be fixed, and she said most Americans believe this.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] Harris also advocated for stricter asylum rules than Biden.[^cite_note-263] Harris supported increasing the number of [U.S. Border Patrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Border_Patrol) agents and accused Trump of being unserious on border security.[^cite_note-264] As vice president, Harris also supported a bipartisan bill that would have funded additional border agents and closed the border if too crowded; the bill was rejected by Trump. Trump called on House and Senate Republicans to kill the bill, arguing it would hurt his and the Republican Party's reelection campaigns and deny them the ability to run on immigration as a campaign issue.[^cite_note-trump-border-biden-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] Harris criticized Trump for his opposition to the bill on the campaign trail,[^cite_note-Politico-2024-262] and promised to sign the bill into law as president.[^cite_note-271]



Trump pledged to finish [the wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border_wall) on the southern border if elected.

Trump stated that if he were elected, he would increase deportations, send the U.S. military to the border, expand [U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Immigration_and_Customs_Enforcement) detentions through workplace raids,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-3-272] deputize local law enforcement to handle border security, increase [U.S. Customs and Border Protection](757040.md) funding, as well as finish building the wall on the southern border.[^cite_note-273] Trump has said he will deport both legal and illegal immigrants.[^cite_note-Oliphant_10042024-12][^cite_note-274] *The New York Times* reported that Trump was considering "an extreme expansion of his first-term crackdown on immigration", such as "preparing to round up undocumented people already in the United States on a vast scale and detain them in sprawling camps while they wait to be expelled".[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-3-272] Trump stated his intention to deport 11 million people through the construction of detention camps and deploy the military,[^cite_note-Time-2024-251] relying on presidential wartime powers under the 18th-century [Alien Enemies Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alien_Enemies_Act).[^cite_note-Ward_10122024-275] Trump made false claims of a "migrant crime wave" that are not supported by data, and provided no evidence to back up his claims.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-Reid_09292024-277]

In regards to his anti-immigrant [nativism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nativism_\(politics\)),[^cite_note-Bender_09252024-278] Trump's tone grew harsher from his previous time as president,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-3-272] and used [fearmongering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearmongering),[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] racial stereotypes,[^cite_note-Bender_09252024-278] and more dehumanizing rhetoric when referring to illegal immigrants. Trump repeatedly called some immigrants subhuman, stating they are "not human", "not people", and "animals",[^cite_note-Reuters-2024-2-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] who will "rape, pillage, thieve, plunder and kill" American citizens,[^cite_note-Oliphant_10042024-12] that they are "stone-cold killers", "monsters", "vile animals", "savages", and "predators" that will "walk into your kitchen, they'll cut your throat",[^cite_note-Gold_1012024-284][^cite_note-Hutzler_09302024-285][^cite_note-Oliphant_10042024-12][^cite_note-Reid_09292024-277] and "grab young girls and slice them up right in front of their parents".[^cite_note-Oliphant_10042024-12] Other rhetoric includes false statements that foreign leaders are deliberately emptying insane asylums to send "prisoners, murderers, drug dealers, mental patients, terrorists" across the southern border as migrants,[^cite_note-286] that they are "building an army" of "fighting age" men to attack Americans "from within",[^cite_note-287] and are the "enemy from within" who are ruining the "fabric" of the country.[^cite_note-Ward_10122024-275] Since fall 2023,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-5-288] Trump claimed that immigrants are "poisoning the blood of our country", which drew comparisons to [racial hygiene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_hygiene) rhetoric used by [white supremacists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_supremacists) and [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md).[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-290][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-5-288][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-5-291] In the 20 rallies that occurred after [Trump's debate with Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_debates#September_10:_Second_presidential_debate_\(ABC,_Philadelphia\)), *[Politico](9172097.md)* cited experts who found that Trump's rhetoric strongly echoed authoritarian and [Nazi ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_ideology); Trump made claims that immigrants are genetically predisposed to commit crimes and have "bad genes".[^cite_note-Ward_10122024-275][^cite_note-Bad_Genes-292]

### Climate change

Main article: [Climate change in the United States](18246585.md)

Further information: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Climate change and energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Climate_change_and_energy), and [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Energy and environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Energy_and_environment)

See also: [Environmental policy of the first Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_policy_of_the_first_Trump_administration), [Climate change denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_denial), and [Inflation Reduction Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_Reduction_Act)

Climate change and energy policy played a role in the 2024 presidential campaign. In 2023, the United States saw a record in crude oil production with over 13.2 million barrels of crude per day, beating the 13 million barrels per day produced at the peak of Trump's presidency.[^cite_note-293] The United States also dealt with supply shocks caused by the [2021–2024 global energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%932024_global_energy_crisis) due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md) and [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-294] An advocate for [environmental justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_justice) to address the impact of climate change on lower-income areas and people of color, Harris supported Biden's climate legislation.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] In 2022, Harris helped pass the Inflation Reduction Act,[^cite_note-The_Hill-2024-295] the largest investment in addressing climate change and clean energy in American history,[^cite_note-296] putting the United States on track to meet emissions reduction targets by 50–52% below 2005 levels by 2030.[^cite_note-297] Harris's campaign stated that she would not support a ban on [fracking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fracking).[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-7-221]

Trump ridiculed the idea of man-made [climate change](5042951.md),[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] and repeatedly referred to his energy policy under the mantra "[drill, baby, drill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drill,_baby,_drill)".[^cite_note-301] Trump said he would increase oil drilling on public lands and offer tax breaks to oil, gas, and coal producers, and stated his goal for the United States to have the lowest cost of electricity and energy of any country in the world.[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] Trump also promised to roll back [electric vehicle](279350.md) initiatives, proposed once again the [United States withdrawal from the Paris Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_withdrawal_from_the_Paris_Agreement), and rescind several environmental regulations.[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302][^cite_note-303] Trump stated his intention to roll back parts of the [Inflation Reduction Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflation_Reduction_Act), Biden's signature domestic legislation.[^cite_note-304] The implementation of Trump's plans would add around 4 billion tons of [carbon dioxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_dioxide) to the atmosphere by 2030, also having effects on the international level. If the policies do not change further, it would add 15 billion tons by 2040 and 27 billion by 2050. Although the exact calculation is difficult, researchers stated: "Regardless of the precise impact, a second Trump term that successfully dismantles [Biden's climate legacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_policy_of_the_Joe_Biden_administration) would likely end any global hopes of keeping [global warming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming) below 1.5C."[^cite_note-305]

### Democracy

Main articles: [Attempts to overturn the 2020 United States presidential election](65905202.md), [Democratic backsliding in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States), and [Election denial movement in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_denial_movement_in_the_United_States)

See also: [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Authoritarian and antidemocratic statements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Authoritarian_and_antidemocratic_statements), [Donald Trump and fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_and_fascism), [Indictments against Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indictments_against_Donald_Trump), and [Project 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_2025)



The election was the first presidential election following Trump's [attempts to overturn the 2020 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempts_to_overturn_the_2020_election) and the [January 6 United States Capitol attack](66307490.md). It also came amidst [Trump's federal indictment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_prosecution_of_Donald_Trump_\(election_obstruction_case\)) for attempting to overturn the 2020 election and involvement in the attack and [racketeering charges for attempting to overturn Biden's victory in Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_election_racketeering_prosecution).

Polling before the election indicated profound dissatisfaction with the state of [American democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_democracy).[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] According to an October 25 [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Corporation)/[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll, 49% of Americans saw Trump as a [fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist), described as "a political extremist who seeks to act as a dictator, disregards individual rights and threatens or uses force against their opponents". Meanwhile, only 22% saw Harris as a fascist by this definition.[^cite_note-309] Some Republicans were concerned that Trump's former [impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_impeachment_trial_of_Donald_Trump) and [four criminal indictments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indictments_against_Donald_Trump) were attempts to influence the election and keep him from office;[^cite_note-310] however, there is no evidence that Trump's criminal trials were "election interference" orchestrated by Biden and the Democratic Party,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-6-26][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-4-80] and Trump also continued to repeat [false claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_claims_of_fraud_in_the_2020_United_States_presidential_election) that the [2020 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election,_2020) was "rigged" and stolen from him.[^cite_note-Associated_Press-2023-2-311]

Trump's 2024 presidential campaign was criticized by legal experts, historians, and political scientists for making increasingly [dehumanizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dehumanizing), violent, and [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian) statements.[^cite_note-Associated_Press-2023-4-312][^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-2-313][^cite_note-The_Guardian-2023-314] Trump's platform called for the vast expansion of presidential powers and the executive branch over every part of the federal government.[^cite_note-315] Trump called for stripping employment protections for thousands of career civil service employees (a provision known as [Schedule F appointment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schedule_F_appointment) that had been adopted by Trump at the end of 2020) and replacing them with political loyalists if deemed an "obstacle to his agenda" within federal agencies, the [United States Intelligence Community](501244.md), [State Department](31975.md), and [Department of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Defense).[^cite_note-316] Trump repeatedly stated his intention to have the [Justice Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Justice_Department) investigate and arrest his domestic political rivals, judges, prosecutors, and witnesses involved in his criminal trials.[^cite_note-vows-120][^cite_note-317][^cite_note-NYMag_Never_Sounded_Like_This-318] Calling the [January 6, 2021, Capitol attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_6,_2021,_Capitol_attack) a "day of love", Trump promised to [pardon those charged for their involvement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_proceedings_in_the_January_6_United_States_Capitol_attack#Trump's_consideration_of_presidential_pardon) and called them "hostages" and "great, great patriots".[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] Trump played down the possibility of violence if he were to lose the 2024 election, but did not rule it out altogether.[^cite_note-IbssaKim2024-121]

Trump's 2024 campaign rhetoric has been described as fascist.[^cite_note-Vox_Fascist-323][^cite_note-New_Yorker_Fascist-324][^cite_note-The_Atlantic_Fascist-325] Trump said his political opponents are a greater threat to the United States than countries such as Russia, China, and North Korea.[^cite_note-The_Washington_Post-2023-326][^cite_note-327] He urged that the U.S. Armed Forces be deployed on American soil to fight "the enemy from within", which—according to Trump—included "radical left lunatics" and Democratic politicians such as [Adam Schiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Schiff).[^cite_note-328] Trump repeatedly voiced support for outlawing political dissent and criticism he considers misleading or challenges his claims to power.[^cite_note-Blake_09242024-329][^cite_note-Kapur_10132024-330] Trump previously tried to have his political rivals prosecuted during his first term.[^cite_note-331]

Harris was tasked by Biden with protecting democracy through voting rights legislation through her work on the [For the People Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/For_the_People_Act). Harris supported efforts to defend election workers and counter [Republican efforts to restrict voting following the 2020 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_efforts_to_restrict_voting_following_the_2020_presidential_election).[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] Harris also stated her intent to pass the [Freedom to Vote Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_to_Vote_Act) and [John Lewis Rights Voting Rights Advancement Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lewis_Rights_Voting_Rights_Advancement_Act) if elected.[^cite_note-332]

A December 2023 [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\)) poll found a record low 28% of Americans reporting that they were satisfied with the way democracy is working in the country.[^cite_note-333] In the weeks before the election, surveys showed that many favored a concession speech from the losing presidential candidate, had broad concerns about the state of democracy and were fearful of future acts of political violence.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337]

Surveys from the [National Election Pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Election_Pool) and [VoteCast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AP_VoteCast) highlight the role the issue of democracy played in the election. According to results provided by the National Election Pool, 34% percent of voters said that the state of democracy mattered most to their vote. The poll found that 25% of those who cast a ballot said that democracy in the U.S. is secure, while 73% said it is threatened.[^cite_note-338] VoteCast found that half of voters identified the future of democracy as the single most important factor for their vote. 9 in 10 Harris voters who said that democracy was the single most important factor expressed concern that electing Trump would bring the country closer to authoritarianism. About 80% of Trump voters who chose democracy as their top issue expressed concern about a Harris presidency becoming authoritarian.[^cite_note-339]

### Economic issues

Main article: [Economy of the United States](32022.md)

Further information: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Economy), [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Economy and trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Economy_and_trade), and [U.S. federal government response to the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._federal_government_response_to_the_COVID-19_pandemic)



[Post-COVID inflation spike in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%932023_inflation_surge_in_the_United_States), with the gray column indicating the [COVID-19 recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_recession_in_the_United_States)

Voters consistently cited the economy as their top issue in the 2024 election.[^cite_note-Pew_Research_Center_2024-340] Following the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), [a global surge in inflation](69605317.md) ensued that raised prices on many goods, although the U.S. inflation rate had declined significantly during 2023 and 2024.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] *The New York Times* reported that both candidates "embraced a vision of a powerful federal government, using its muscle to intervene in markets in pursuit of a stronger and more prosperous economy".[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-8-344] *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that economists found Trump's proposed policies created a greater risk of stoking inflation and generating higher budget deficits, relative to the Harris plan.[^cite_note-345] Twenty-three [Nobel Prize](21201.md)-winning economists signed a letter characterizing the Harris economic plan as "vastly superior" to the Trump plan.[^cite_note-346] Trump's designated government efficiency leader [Elon Musk](909036.md) said in October that he expected Trump's plan would involve more than \$2 trillion in federal spending cuts and would cause "some temporary hardship."[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] Harris ran on a pro-union platform.[^cite_note-349] She promoted the passage of the [Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure_Investment_and_Jobs_Act), funding for small business, and previously supported an act as senator to provide a \$6,000 tax credit for middle and low-income families.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] Harris promised to address [price gouging](879011.md), bring down costs, ban hidden fees and late charges from financial institutions, limit "unfair" rent increases and cap prescription drug costs, which she said would "lower costs and save many middle-class families thousands of dollars a year".[^cite_note-350]

*The New York Times* described Harris's economic policy as embracing "the idea that the federal government must act aggressively to foster competition and correct distortions in private markets". Harris proposed raising taxes on corporations and high-earners to fund services for the lower and middle classes and reduce the deficit.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-8-344] Harris stated she supported increasing the top tier [capital gains tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_gains_tax) rate to 28%, up from 20% and lower than Biden's proposed 39.6%. Harris stated her support for a Billionaire Minimum Income Tax, increasing the tax on [stock buybacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_buybacks) to 4%, and a ten-fold tax reduction for small business ranging from \$5,000 to \$50,000 in relief.[^cite_note-351] Harris also supported efforts to create a tax on unrealized gains for those with more than a \$100 million in net worth if they do not pay a minimum 25% tax rate on their income inclusive of unrealized gains so long as 80% of said wealth is in tradeable assets. The plan would impact a small percentage of the wealthy in the United States, and *[Axios](52436164.md)* reported most tech founders and investors would be spared.[^cite_note-352] Harris also announced support for restoring the corporate tax rate to 28% among several other tax proposals to raise taxes and close loopholes for corporations and the wealthy that would bring in \$5 trillion in additional revenue over 10 years.[^cite_note-353] Harris proposed tax breaks to companies delivering economic benefit, such as manufacturing technologies that [mitigate climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitigate_climate_change) and building [affordable housing](3531662.md), and proposed a ban on corporate price gouging to "help the food industry become more competitive".[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-8-344] Harris also expressed support for student debt relief,[^cite_note-CNN-2024-2-354] and said she supported raising the [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-nytaug11-355]

In response to the [housing crisis in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_crisis_in_the_United_States), Harris said she would increase home construction to reduce housing costs, arguing that it negatively impacts the economy and hurts working-class families. Harris proposed directing \$40 billion to construction companies to build starter homes, and promised to send \$25,000 in down-payment assistance to every first time home buyer.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-8-344] Harris said she would urge Congress to enforce fair housing laws and pass a bill to bar property owners from using services that "coordinate" rents through the passage of the Preventing the Algorithmic Facilitation of Rental Housing Cartels Act, and also call on Congress to pass the Stop Predatory Investing Act by removing tax benefits to Wall Street firms that buy up large numbers of single-family homes.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] Trump proposed further individual and corporate tax cuts beyond the [2017 Tax Cuts and Jobs Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Tax_Cuts_and_Jobs_Act).[^cite_note-358] Trump argued that keeping taxes low for the wealthy increases job creation,[^cite_note-359] and that these policies coupled with a crackdown in illegal immigration and reduction in inflation would help the middle class.[^cite_note-CNN-2024-2-354] Trump said he would reduce regulation of business through the creation of an efficiency commission led by Musk,[^cite_note-360] along with reducing environmental regulation. By October 2024, Musk was Trump's second-largest individual campaign donor.[^cite_note-361] Trump said deporting millions of immigrants would bring housing prices down, although most economists argue it could raise prices by removing construction workers who use less real estate.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-8-344][^cite_note-362] Trump and Harris support not taxing tips for at least hospitality and service workers.[^cite_note-nytaug11-355][^cite_note-363] Trump suggested that he would abolish the federal income tax and replace it with tariffs. In June 2024, Trump discussed the idea of eliminating the income tax in a private meeting with Republican politicians.[^cite_note-364] In October 2024, Trump suggested that he would scrap the income tax if he wins, pointing out that tariffs were the main sources of revenue in the 19th century.[^cite_note-365]

Trump's stated trade policy involves the United States decoupling from the global economy and having the country become more self-contained and exerting its power through individual trade dealings. This would be attempted largely through a universal baseline tariff,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-6-366] set from 10% to 20% on all imports,[^cite_note-Axios-2024-367][^cite_note-368] with increased penalties if trade partners manipulate their currency or engage in unfair trade practices.[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] Trump called for 100% tariffs on cars made outside the United States and a minimum 60% tariff on Chinese goods.[^cite_note-Axios-2024-367] Trump stated his plans to urge Congress to pass a Trump Reciprocal Trade Act to bestow presidential authority to impose a reciprocal tariff on any country that imposed one on the United States.[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* reported in January 2024 that Trump was preparing for a massive trade war.[^cite_note-trump-china-trade-war-369] Trump's trade policies were described as [protectionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionist),[^cite_note-370] neo-[mercantilist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercantilist), or [autarkist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autarkist),[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2023-6-366][^cite_note-371] and increasing inflation became a more common critique of Trump's economic plans.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] In June 2024, 16 [Nobel Prize in Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Economics) laureates signed an open letter arguing that Trump's fiscal and trade policies coupled with efforts to limit the [Federal Reserve](10819.md)'s independence would reignite inflation in the United States.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] [Moody's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moody's_Corporation),[^cite_note-379] as well as most economists surveyed by *The Wall Street Journal* in July 2024, predicted that inflation would be worse under Trump than Biden, a result due in part to tariffs, a crack down on illegal immigration, and larger deficits.[^cite_note-380] Trump incorrectly insisted foreign exporters pay tariffs imposed by the U.S. government; American importers pay tariffs on goods upon arrival at U.S. ports, meaning tariffs are taxes that raise prices for imported products Americans buy.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-WD-383] One non-partisan analysis estimated the proposed tariffs would cost \$1,700 per year for the average household.[^cite_note-WD-383] The [Committee for a Responsible Federal Budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_for_a_Responsible_Federal_Budget) found that Trump's plans would grow the national debt at roughly twice the rate of Harris' plan,[^cite_note-384] while the [Institute on Taxation and Economic Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_on_Taxation_and_Economic_Policy) found Trump's plan would only benefit the top 5% of earners.[^cite_note-385]

### Education

Main article: [Education in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_the_United_States)

Further information: [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Education), [2020s controversies around critical race theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020s_controversies_around_critical_race_theory), and [Parental rights movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parental_rights_movement)

Trump pledged to terminate the [U.S. Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Education),[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] claiming it has been infiltrated by "radical zealots and Marxists".[^cite_note-388] At the [American Federation of Teachers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Federation_of_Teachers) national convention, Harris attacked [recent efforts to ban books in school libraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_banning_in_the_United_States_\(2021%E2%80%93present\)).[^cite_note-389] She also previously called for raising teachers' wages.[^cite_note-390]

### Healthcare issues

Further information: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Healthcare), and [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Healthcare and social services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Healthcare_and_social_services)

Unlike previous elections, healthcare reform played a much more minor role in the 2024 presidential election.[^cite_note-391] Harris stated that she no longer supported a [single-payer healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singlepayer_healthcare_in_the_United_States) system as she had in 2020.[^cite_note-CNN-2024-392] Instead, she said she intended to protect and expand items legislated during the [Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Barack_Obama) and [Biden administrations](65732174.md). She said she would "maintain and grow" the [Affordable Care Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_Care_Act), while Trump said that he would replace it with his own healthcare plan.[^cite_note-393] Harris also supported limiting yearly out-of-pocket drug costs for seniors, and expanding the \$35 cap on insulin for seniors on [Medicare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicare_\(United_States\)) to younger individuals in the program as well.[^cite_note-The_Hill-2024-295] Generally, both candidates supported using the government to rein in prescription drug costs.[^cite_note-394] Trump suggested he was open to cutting entitlement programs, such as [Social Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Security_\(United_States\)) and [Medicare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicare_\(United_States\)), part of an effort to "\[cut] waste" as described by his campaign. During his first term, several budget proposals did suggest cuts to the programs.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] Additionally, Vance and Speaker of the House [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) suggested cuts to the ACA, including around [pre-existing conditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-existing_condition), were part of Trump's plan.[^cite_note-397] After [Robert F. Kennedy Jr.](522298.md) dropped out of the race and endorsed Trump, Kennedy advocated for his "[Make America Healthy Again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Make_America_Healthy_Again)" agenda, pledging to combat the upward trend in chronic disease patients, with Trump saying Kennedy would "go wild" regarding policy on food and medicines.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399]

### Foreign policy

Further information: [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § Foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#Foreign_policy), and [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § Foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#Foreign_policy)



[Joe Biden](145422.md) signing [Executive Order 14065](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Executive%20Order%2014065) in February 2022 in response to Russia's imminent invasion of Ukraine. The United States has given [billions worth of military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) to Ukraine following the Russian invasion of the country in 2022.

The [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md), the [Gaza war](74998519.md), and [Chinese expansionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_expansionism) were some of the main foreign policy issues of the election. Harris signaled she would generally follow Biden's foreign policy on [NATO](21133.md) and Ukraine, supporting both in the aftermath of the Russian invasion.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247][^cite_note-Reuters-2024-3-400] A supporter of the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) to the Israeli-Palestinian conflict,[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247][^cite_note-401] Harris advocated for "de-risking" from China, a policy that encourages reducing Western economic dependence on China.[^cite_note-402] Harris was expected to continue deepening American alliances in Asia and the Pacific with the intention of curbing China's rising power both economically and militarily.[^cite_note-403]

Trump's 2024 campaign promoted an [isolationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolationist), "[America First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First)" foreign policy.[^cite_note-NYT_NATO_Withdrawal-404][^cite_note-405] Trump said that America's allies "treat us actually worse than our so-called enemies", and added: "We protect them and then they screw us on trade." He also vowed to impose [tariffs](55551.md) on trade partners; economists said this could spark [trade wars](441392.md).[^cite_note-406] He promised to "fundamentally reevaluate" NATO, shifting the country's defense spending from Europe towards Asia.[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] Although NATO members are [obliged to defend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_5_of_the_North_Atlantic_Treaty) any other member who is attacked, Trump said he would encourage Russia to "do whatever the hell they want" to NATO allies that did not spend enough on defense.[^cite_note-407] NATO Secretary-General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg) responded: "Any suggestion that allies will not defend each other undermines all of our security."[^cite_note-408] Trump vowed that even before he was inaugurated,[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] he would negotiate an end to the Russo-Ukrainian war in one day.[^cite_note-NYT_NATO_Withdrawal-404] He promised to quickly cut the amount of military and financial aid to Ukraine,[^cite_note-409] and make Europeans reimburse the United States the cost of rebuilding its old stockpiles;[^cite_note-AP_Trump_Plans-302] however, most of the money for Ukraine actually goes to American factories that make weapons and military equipment.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] Trump previously said he might recognize [Russia's illegal annexation of Crimea](42228673.md),[^cite_note-413] and suggested the [2022 invasion](79400268.md) could have been prevented by Ukraine giving up parts of its own country to Russia.[^cite_note-NYT_NATO_Withdrawal-404] Trump promised a tougher stance against China,[^cite_note-trump-china-trade-war-369] and at the same time questioned whether the United States should defend [Taiwan](25734.md).[^cite_note-414] Trump suggested withdrawing troops from South Korea if it does not pay more to support American troops there.[^cite_note-Time-2024-251]

#### Gaza war views



Demonstrators at the [March on Washington for Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_on_Washington_for_Gaza) in January 2024

Further information: [Gaza war protest vote movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protest_vote_movements) and [Gaza war protests in the United States](75347645.md)

Polling indicated that the majority of voters support a ceasefire and American mediation in the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-415] According to a [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll in March 2024, 52% of Americans supported stopping weapons shipments to Israel, coming largely from Americans who voted for Biden in 2020 (62% support) and people who did not vote in 2020 (60%). 55% of Americans who voted for Trump in 2020 opposed halting weapons shipments, while 30% supported the measure.[^cite_note-416] Republicans generally supported arms to Israel, while Democrats were divided on the issue.[^cite_note-417]

Harris largely continued Biden's approach to the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md),[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera-2024-418] although she was seen as more sympathetic to [Palestinians](23267.md) than Biden or Trump.[^cite_note-Reuters-2024-3-400] During the Gaza war, Harris supported Israel's offensive, saying "the threat [Hamas](13913.md) poses to the people of Israel must be eliminated".[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247] By March 2024, Harris was increasingly critical of Israel, calling for a ceasefire and [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution), and opposing the [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), though still supporting the war overall.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-3-247][^cite_note-Israel-419] In the Democratic primaries, the [Uncommitted National Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncommitted_National_Movement) led a protest campaign against Biden and later Harris over the war, calling for a ceasefire and [arms embargo on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_embargo_on_Israel). It received over 700,000 votes and 36 delegates.[^cite_note-dnc-uncommitted-arab-420] Harris engaged with Uncommitted and Arab-American leaders, but refused to change her position. Harris argued for continued weapons shipments to Israel, saying it had a right to defend itself.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] Uncommitted ultimately encouraged its supporters to vote for Harris, though many were reluctant.[^cite_note-Weisman_1082024-423]

Trump consistently supported Israel, though presented himself as the "candidate of peace".[^cite_note-TI-424][^cite_note-fast-425] Trump also supported giving weapons to Israel, likely with "no strings attached", saying that it must "finish the problem".[^cite_note-426] Trump was seen by some as more pro-Israel and less sympathetic to Palestine than Biden or Harris.[^cite_note-TI-424] Trump took a hardline stance against pro-Palestinian protests, telling donors he would "crush" them and deport non-citizen protestors to "set the movement back 25 or 30 years".[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] At times, Trump was critical of Israel's conduct, saying Israel should "get it over with ... get back to peace and stop killing people".[^cite_note-fast-425][^cite_note-429] However, he pledged to end the war in Gaza through negotiation and bring peace to the Middle East.[^cite_note-430] Trump called Biden's [airstrikes on Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US%E2%80%93UK_airstrikes_on_Yemen) "crazy" and suggested negotiating with the [Houthis](17240332.md).[^cite_note-431]

### LGBTQ rights

Main articles: [LGBTQ rights in the United States](3510770.md) and [Transgender rights in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_the_United_States)

Further information: [Kamala Harris 2024 presidential campaign § LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris_2024_presidential_campaign#LGBTQ_rights), [Donald Trump 2024 presidential campaign § LGBTQ rights and civil rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_2024_presidential_campaign#LGBTQ_rights_and_civil_rights), and [2020s anti-LGBTQ movement in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020s_anti-LGBTQ_movement_in_the_United_States)

In the 2020s, conservative politicians in state legislatures introduced a growing number of bills that restrict the rights of [LGBTQ](78174325.md) people, especially [transgender](19904525.md) people.[^cite_note-432] A strong supporter of [LGBTQ rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights),[^cite_note-433] Harris denounced legislative attacks on transgender rights in states across the country.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] Exit polls showed that Harris had an 86%-12% advantage over Trump among voters who say that they are gay, lesbian, bisexual or transgender. Harris' lead among this group is the largest for a 21st-century Democratic presidential candidate with those reporting to be gay, lesbian, bisexual or transgender (2016–2024) and gay/lesbian/bisexual respondents (2000–2008). Exit poll results for the 2012 election are not available.[^cite_note-cnn-national-results-436][^cite_note-CNN2020-437][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] Trump promised to roll back policies regarding transgender individuals.[^cite_note-Axios-2023-442] Harris and Walz campaigned as supporters of LGBTQ+ rights.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444] Trump stated he would rescind Biden's [Title IX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Title_IX) protections "on day one" for transgender students using bathrooms, locker rooms, and pronouns that align with their [gender identities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity).[^cite_note-445] Trump stated he would enact a federal law that would recognize only two genders and claimed that being transgender is a concept only recently manufactured by "the radical left".[^cite_note-446] Trump previously withdrew Title IX provisions that allowed transgender youth to have access to the bathrooms of their choice, and he attempted to roll-back several transgender-related policies in the [Affordable Care Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_Care_Act).[^cite_note-Axios-2023-442] Trump repeated a false claim that children undergo transgender surgery while at school, without parental knowledge or consent.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448] According to [Future Forward PAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_Forward_PAC), a derivative of his campaign's "[Kamala is for they/them, President Trump is for you](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_is_for_they/them,_President_Trump_is_for_you)" [attack ad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_ad) (with commentary by [Charlamagne tha God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlamagne_tha_God) spliced in) was one of the most effective of the campaign, shifting the race 2.7 percentage points in favor of Trump after viewers watched it.[^cite_note-nyt20241107-449] However, findings from an [RCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randomized_controlled_trial) study released by Ground Media/[GLAAD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD) said that the ad did not have an impact on who viewers intended to vote for.[^cite_note-\:2-450][^cite_note-\:3-451]

## Election-related violence

### Assassination attempts on Trump

Main articles: [Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Donald_Trump_in_Pennsylvania) and [Attempted assassination of Donald Trump in Florida](77879114.md)



Trump at the [RNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Republican_National_Convention) standing alongside the fire department uniform of Corey Comperatore, who was killed during the attempted assassination of Trump

On July 13, 2024, Trump survived [an assassination attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Donald_Trump_in_Pennsylvania) while addressing a [campaign rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campaign_rally) near [Butler, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler,_Pennsylvania).[^cite_note-Pennsylvania_Assassination-452] Trump was shot and wounded on his right ear by 20-year-old [Thomas Crooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Crooks),[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-9-453][^cite_note-454] who fired eight rounds with an [AR-15–style rifle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AR-15%E2%80%93style_rifle) from the roof of a building approximately 400 feet (120 metres) from the stage; the shots killed one audience member and critically injured two others.[^cite_note-The_New_York_Times-2024-9-453] Seconds later, Crooks was shot and killed by the [U.S. Secret Service's counter-sniper team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secret_Service's_counter-sniper_team).[^cite_note-455] On September 11, 2024, a bipartisan Senate report identified technical issues and other preventable mistakes by the Secret Service during the event.[^cite_note-nbc25-456]

On September 15, 2024, Trump survived [a second assassination attempt](77879114.md) at [Trump International Golf Club](53109904.md) in [West Palm Beach, Florida](109634.md). The perpetrator, 58-year-old [Ryan Wesley Routh](77880898.md), took position in nearby shrubbery holding an [SKS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SKS)-style rifle just outside the fence of the golf course.[^cite_note-DOJ_Detention_Memo-457] After hiding there for nearly 12 hours, Routh pointed his weapon through the fence line, approximately 400 yards (370 m) away from Trump. A Secret Service agent noticed this and fired four rounds towards Routh, who fled the scene and was later captured in [Martin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_County,_Florida).[^cite_note-458] No injuries were reported. Routh was later indicted on five federal charges and three state charges, all of which Routh pleaded not guilty to. His trial began on September 8, 2025.[^cite_note-WPBF-459] Two weeks later on September 23, Routh was found guilty on all federal counts,[^cite_note-\:0-460] where he then grabbed a pen and attempted to stab himself in the neck, before being tackled by U.S. marshals.[^cite_note-verdict-461] Routh was sentenced to life without parole on February 4, 2026.[^cite_note-\:0-460][^cite_note-462]

### Violence towards election workers

Since the 2020 election and continuing into the 2024 election, the [election denial movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_denial_movement_in_the_United_States) prompted thousands of [death threats](13262036.md) directed at election workers, officials, and their families, with some receiving letters laced with [fentanyl](141915.md).[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464] As of March 2024, the [Department of Justice's](52563.md) Election Threats Task Force had charged 20 people with threat-related crimes.[^cite_note-465] In September 2024, suspicious packages were sent to state election officials in several states, which resulted in evacuations. The inclusion of white powder in most of the packages mirrored the [2001 anthrax attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_anthrax_attacks); the substance in Oklahoma packages was identified as flour.[^cite_note-466] Threats led some election workers to resign, and affected recruitment of temporary [poll workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poll_workers).[^cite_note-VD-467][^cite_note-HT-468] In locations where funds were available,[^cite_note-HT-468] efforts to protect election workers involved [active shooter training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_shooter_training), provision of [first aid kits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_aid_kit) and [Narcan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narcan), [bulletproof vests](149058.md), [bulletproof glass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulletproof_glass), [metal detectors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metal_detector), armed guards, [police snipers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_sniper), and [drones](58900.md).[^cite_note-VD-467]

### Violence towards voters

On October 30, 2024, an 18-year-old man in [Jacksonville, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville,_Florida), was arrested for [aggravated assault](1466.md) and [improper exhibition of a dangerous weapon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improper_exhibition_of_a_dangerous_weapon) after brandishing a machete at two women outside an early voting center. He, along with seven other teenagers, allegedly approached and antagonized members of the opposing political party as they were demonstrating. Neither the teenager's nor the women's political parties were disclosed, although later posts by the Duval Democratic Party described the teenager's party as a "group of young men carrying Trump flags".[^cite_note-469] On November 1, 2024, a voter wearing a "[Let's Go Brandon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Let's_Go_Brandon)" hat was reportedly struck by a poll worker after a verbal altercation over his hat at an [Orangeburg County, South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orangeburg_County,_South_Carolina), polling location.[^cite_note-470] Also on November 1, a man in [Bath, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bath,_New_York), was arrested for assaulting someone in a supermarket for wearing a Trump hat.[^cite_note-471]

### Arson of ballot boxes

In late October 2024, multiple fires were reported at ballot drop boxes in [Portland, Oregon](23503.md), and [Vancouver, Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver,_Washington). The fires damaged hundreds of ballots, requiring election officials to identify and offer new ballots to those affected by the fires. Prior to the fires, the [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) and the [Department of Homeland Security](58236.md) had issued a bulletin raising concerns that "election-related grievances" could motivate domestic extremist activity and that ballot drop boxes could potentially be "attractive targets".[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] In [Phoenix, Arizona](49121.md), a fire was started in a mail collection box, destroying some ballots and other mail. A suspect was arrested and claimed that the fire was unrelated to the election.[^cite_note-474]

#### Polling accuracy

Following polling inaccuracies in connection with the 2020 presidential election, pollsters took steps to avoid similar errors in 2024.[^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] Pollsters used different approaches and methodologies for the 2024 election than what they had used previously.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479]

A report from the [American Association for Public Opinion Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Association_for_Public_Opinion_Research) noted that, overall, polls correctly indicated that "the race between Kamala Harris and Donald Trump was close, in both pivotal swing states and the nation as a whole." The report noted that pre-election polls tended to underestimate Republican vote shares, although to a lesser degree than in 2016 and 2020. The polls also failed to reliably measure the preferences of Republican voters in GOP-leaning areas, Hispanic voters, and those who had voted in 2024 but not 2020.[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481]

Despite efforts by pollsters to improve survey accuracy, national polls underestimated Trump's support once again in 2024.[^cite_note-482] In 2016, national polls were fairly accurate; however, Trump overperformed the polls in the Democratic-leaning Rust Belt states of Michigan, Pennsylvania, and Wisconsin, leading to his Electoral College victory. In 2020, polls had overestimated Biden's margin over Trump by approximately 4% in competitive states.[^cite_note-483] In 2024, pollsters underestimated Trump's support by smaller margins than they did in 2016 and 2020,[^cite_note-484] and their underestimation of that support was within the realm of a [normal polling error](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normal_polling_error).[^cite_note-Morris_2024-485][^cite_note-Warren_2024-486] Going into the election, most polls showed the race to be neck and neck and within the margin of error.[^cite_note-487] In every swing state, Trump outperformed his final polling numbers by approximately 3%, which is in line with a typical margin of error.[^cite_note-488] Compared with the 2020 polls, the margin of error in 2024 in swing states was lower and high-quality national polls were more accurate.[^cite_note-Warren_2024-486]

Polling averages vastly underestimated Trump's strength in both safe red and safe blue states.[^cite_note-Morris_2024-485][^cite_note-489] Florida and Texas were both projected to go for Trump by about 7%; he won each of them by about 13%.[^cite_note-Morris_2024-485] Pollster [Ann Selzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ann_Selzer) released a poll in Iowa that had Harris winning the state by 3%,[^cite_note-490] only for Trump to take the state by 13%.[^cite_note-Morris_2024-485] On the other hand, New Jersey was projected to be a safe state for Harris but most news stations waited until 90% of the vote was in before calling it for her, as she was only leading by 5%.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492]

## Debates

Main article: [2024 United States presidential debates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_debates)

Biden and Trump agreed to hold debates on [CNN](62028.md) on June 27 and [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) on September 10.[^cite_note-493] [Biden and Trump debated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Joe_Biden%E2%80%93Donald_Trump_presidential_debate) on June 27, 2024, in [Atlanta](3138.md), [Georgia](48830.md).[^cite_note-494] After [Biden suspended his re-election campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_Joe_Biden_from_the_2024_United_States_presidential_election) on July 21, 2024,[^cite_note-495] Harris became the Democratic nominee and debated Trump on September 10, 2024, in [Philadelphia](50585.md), [Pennsylvania](23332.md).[^cite_note-496]

## Results

Joint session of Congress certifying the election results alongside Kamala Harris; January 6, 2025

Trump won the [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College) with 312 electoral votes to Harris' 226.[^cite_note-497] He prevailed in all of the seven [swing states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state) ([Pennsylvania](23332.md), [Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_\(U.S._State\)), [North Carolina](21650.md), [Michigan](18859.md), [Arizona](21883824.md), [Wisconsin](33127.md), and [Nevada](21216.md)).[^cite_note-auto-498] In addition, Trump won the national [popular vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_presidential_elections_by_popular_vote_margin) with a [plurality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plurality_\(voting\)) of 49.8%;[^cite_note-499] Harris received 48.3%.[^cite_note-500]

[Cedric Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedric_Richmond), co-chair the Harris campaign, announced on election night that Harris would not be speaking as originally planned.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] In the early hours of November 6, the day after the election, Trump was projected to win the election, and he declared victory at his [Mar-a-Lago estate](1452754.md).[^cite_note-503] Harris later [conceded](2538448.md) to Trump via a phone call, and publicly conceded the election to Trump that afternoon during a speech at [Howard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_University).[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-reutersnov7-505]

### Close states

The seven [swing states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state) in the 2024 election were the [Rust Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_Belt) states of [Wisconsin](33127.md), [Michigan](18859.md), and [Pennsylvania](23332.md), as well as the [Sun Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Belt) states of [Arizona](21883824.md), [Georgia](48830.md), [Nevada](21216.md), and [North Carolina](21650.md).[^cite_note-BBC-2024-557]

States where the margin of victory was under 1 percentage point (10 electoral votes; all won by Trump):

1. **Wisconsin, 0.86% (29,397 votes) – 10 electoral votes**

States/districts where the margin of victory was between 1 and 5 percentage points (87 electoral votes; 72 won by Trump, 15 won by Harris):

1. **Michigan, 1.42% (80,103 votes) – 15 electoral votes**

2. **Pennsylvania, 1.71% (120,266 votes) – 19 electoral votes** ([tipping-point state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tipping-point_state))

3. **Georgia, 2.20% (115,100 votes) – 16 electoral votes**

4. **New Hampshire, 2.78% (22,965 votes) – 4 electoral votes**

5. **Nevada, 3.10% (46,008 votes) – 6 electoral votes**

6. **North Carolina, 3.21% (183,046 votes) – 16 electoral votes**

7. **Minnesota, 4.24% (137,947 votes) – 10 electoral votes**

8. **Nebraska's 2nd congressional district, 4.59% (14,636 votes) – 1 electoral vote**

States/districts where the margin of victory was between 5% and 10% (46 electoral votes; 12 won by Trump, 34 by Harris):

1. **Arizona, 5.53% (187,382 votes) – 11 electoral votes**

2. **Virginia, 5.78% (260,310 votes) – 13 electoral votes**

3. **New Jersey, 5.91% (252,498 votes) – 14 electoral votes**

4. **New Mexico, 6.00% (55,411 votes) – 5 electoral votes**

5. **Maine, 6.94% (57,514 votes) – 2 electoral votes**

6. **Maine's 2nd congressional district, 9.03% (33,297 votes) – 1 electoral vote**

**Red** denotes states or congressional districts won by Republican Donald Trump; **Blue** denotes those won by Democrat Kamala Harris.
